<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every bounded complex-linear unital algebra homomorphism from a unital complex <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra into the bounded operators on an arbitrary Hilbert space is similar to a $`*`$-homomorphism. This resolves Kadison's similarity conjecture positively. We also obtain one universal hyperreflexivity constant for all unital von Neumann algebras on arbitrary complex Hilbert spaces.

- **stmt-thm-similarity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-similarity)): Let $A$ be any unital complex $C^*$-algebra, let $H$ be any complex Hilbert space, and let $\pi:A\to\mathcal B(H)$ be a bounded, complex-linear, unital algebra homomorphism. There exists an operator $S\in\mathcal B(H)$, invertible with bounded inverse, such that $$\rho(a)=S\pi(a)S^{-1}\qquad(a\in A)$$ is a $*$-homomorphism. In particular, $\rho(a^*)=\rho(a)^*$ for every $a\in A$.
  - Explanation: Every bounded unital complex-linear algebra homomorphism from a unital complex C*-algebra is similar to an adjoint-preserving homomorphism by a bounded invertible operator.
- **stmt-thm-universal** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-universal)): There is an absolute constant $C<\infty$ such that, for every complex Hilbert space $K$, every unital von Neumann algebra $P\subset\mathcal B(K)$, every $Y\in\mathcal B(K)$, every integer $h\ge1$, and every $X\in M_h(P)$, $$\label{eq:universal} \\|[Y^{(h)},X]\\|\le C\,g_P(Y)\,\\|X\\|.$$ Here $M_h(P)$ acts naturally on $K^h$, and $[T,U]=TU-UT$.
  - Explanation: Matrix commutators are controlled by the scalar commutator seminorm with a universal constant.
- **stmt-cor-hyperreflexivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-hyperreflexivity)): Let $H$ be any complex Hilbert space and let $M\subset\mathcal B(H)$ be a unital von Neumann algebra. For $T\in\mathcal B(H)$, set $$\alpha_M(T)=\sup\{\\|(1-e)Te\\|:e\in M',\ e=e^*=e^2\}.$$ Then, with the absolute constant $C$ from Theorem <a href="#thm:universal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:universal">2</a>, $$\label{eq:universal-hyperreflexivity} \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(T,M)\le 2C\,\alpha_M(T)\qquad(T\in\mathcal B(H)).$$ Thus every such $M$ is hyperreflexive with constant at most $2C$, independently of the algebra, its representation, and the Hilbert space. No optimality of this constant is asserted.
  - Explanation: The off-diagonal compression seminorm bounds distance to any unital von Neumann algebra uniformly.
- **stmt-lem-row-column** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-row-column)): Every bounded rectangular derivation satisfies $$\max\{\\|\Delta\\|_{\mathrm{row}},\\|\Delta\\|_{\mathrm{col}}\} \le c_{\rm row}\\|\Delta\\|. \label{eq:rectangular-row-column}$$
  - Explanation: Bounded rectangular derivations have uniformly bounded row and column amplification norms.
- **stmt-lem-cb-implementation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cb-implementation)): Let $\sigma,\lambda,\Delta$ satisfy <a href="#eq:rectangular-derivation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rectangular-derivation">[eq:rectangular-derivation]</a>. If $\Delta$ is completely bounded, then some $V\in\mathcal B(K,F)$ satisfies $$\Delta(a)=V\sigma(a)-\lambda(a)V\qquad(a\in A). \label{eq:rectangular-implementation}$$ If $d=\\|\Delta\\|>0$, one may choose $$\\|V\\|\le d\bigl(1+\\|\Delta\\|_{\mathrm{cb}}/d\bigr)^2.$$
  - Explanation: Completely bounded rectangular derivations admit a spatial implementer with a controlled norm.
- **stmt-lem-cyclic-factorization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cyclic-factorization)): Let $P\subset\mathcal B(K)$ be a unital von Neumann algebra with a cyclic vector. For every integer $h\ge1$ and every unit vector $\xi=(\xi_1,\ldots,\xi_h)\in K^h$, there are a unit vector $\zeta\in K$ and a contraction $v=(v_i)\in M_{h,1}(P)$ such that $\xi_i=v_i\zeta$ for every $i$.
  - Explanation: In a cyclic representation, an amplified unit vector factors through a unit vector and a contractive column in the represented algebra.
- **stmt-lem-cyclic-implementation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cyclic-implementation)): Let $A$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra, let $\sigma:A\to\mathcal B(K)$ and $\lambda:A\to\mathcal B(F)$ be $*$-representations, and suppose that $\sigma$ has a cyclic vector. Every bounded rectangular derivation $\Delta:A\to\mathcal B(K,F)$ is completely bounded, with $$\\|\Delta\\|_{\mathrm{cb}}\le4c_{\rm row}\\|\Delta\\|. \label{eq:cyclic-cb}$$ Moreover there is $V\in\mathcal B(K,F)$ such that $$\Delta(a)=V\sigma(a)-\lambda(a)V\quad(a\in A),\qquad \\|V\\|\le c_0\\|\Delta\\|. \label{eq:cyclic-implementation}$$
  - Explanation: A bounded rectangular derivation with cyclic domain is completely bounded and spatially implemented.
- **stmt-thm-kirchberg** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-kirchberg)): For a unital $C^*$-algebra $A$, the following properties are equivalent.* 1. *Every bounded unital homomorphism $\pi:A\to\mathcal B(H)$, on any Hilbert space $H$, is similar by a bounded invertible operator to a $*$-homomorphism.* 2. *For every faithful unital $*$-representation $\sigma:A\to\mathcal B(K)$, on any Hilbert space $K$, every bounded linear map $\Delta:A\to\mathcal B(K)$ satisfying $$\Delta(ab)=\Delta(a)\sigma(b)+\sigma(a)\Delta(b)$$ has the form $\Delta(a)=V\sigma(a)-\sigma(a)V$ for some $V\in\mathcal B(K)$.
  - Explanation: The similarity property is equivalent to implementation of bounded derivations in every faithful unital adjoint-preserving representation.
- **stmt-thm-free-corner** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-free-corner)): Let $\mathcal L=D*A_0$ be as above, let $t=1/N$ for an integer $N\geq2$, and suppose that $G\in\mathcal B(L^2(\mathcal L))$ commutes with $L_D$. Suppose that $\epsilon\geq0$ and $$\label{eq:free-corner-hypothesis} \\|[G,L_x]\\|\leq\epsilon\\|x\\| \qquad (x\in e\mathcal L e, \ e\in D\text{ a projection with }\tau(e)=t).$$ If $s=s^*=s^{-1}\in A_0$ and $\tau(s)=0$, then $$\label{eq:free-corner-conclusion} \left\\|P_{L^2(A_0)}[G,L_s]\big\|_{L^2(A_0)}\right\\| \leq c_1\epsilon, \qquad c_1=6\pi^2(1+4c_0).$$
  - Explanation: Small-corner commutator control implies a uniform compressed commutator bound against a centered symmetry in the other free-product algebra.
- **stmt-lem-free-factor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-free-factor)): One has $D'\cap\mathcal L=\mathbb C1$. In particular, $\mathcal L$ is a ${\rm II}_1$ factor.
  - Explanation: The relative commutant of the increasing-matrix factor in the free product is scalar, so the free product is a finite factor.
- **stmt-lem-free-symmetrization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-free-symmetrization)): In proving Theorem <a href="#thm:free-corner" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:free-corner">9</a>, one may suppose in addition that $G$ commutes with $W_u$ for every $u\in\bigcup_j\mathcal U(D_j)$. This replacement preserves the compressed commutator in <a href="#eq:free-corner-conclusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:free-corner-conclusion">[eq:free-corner-conclusion]</a> exactly, and preserves <a href="#eq:free-corner-hypothesis" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:free-corner-hypothesis">[eq:free-corner-hypothesis]</a> for both $G$ and $G^*$.
  - Explanation: Unitary averaging preserves the target compression and corner bounds while imposing the required conjugation symmetries.
- **stmt-lem-invariant-intertwiners** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-invariant-intertwiners)): Let $k,l\geq0$, and let $$Y:E\otimes(E^\circ)^{\otimes k} \longrightarrow E\otimes(E^\circ)^{\otimes l}$$ be bounded. Suppose that $Y$ intertwines the left $D$ actions and that $$Y C_u^{\otimes(k+1)}=C_u^{\otimes(l+1)}Y \qquad(u\in\bigcup_j\mathcal U(D_j)).$$ Then $Y=0$ if $k\ne l$. If $k=l$, there are scalars $(a_\sigma)_{\sigma\in S_k}$ such that $$Y=I_E\otimes\sum_{\sigma\in S_k}a_\sigma U_\sigma, \qquad U_\sigma(x_1\otimes\cdots\otimes x_k) =x_{\sigma^{-1}(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{\sigma^{-1}(k)}.$$ For $k=0$ the sum consists of the scalar multiple of the identity corresponding to the empty permutation.
  - Explanation: Invariant intertwiners vanish between distinct tensor levels and are linear combinations of tail permutations on equal levels.
- **stmt-lem-word-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-word-structure)): The symmetrized operator $G$ preserves every level $\mathcal H_k$ in <a href="#eq:word-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:word-decomposition">[eq:word-decomposition]</a>. There are operators $B_k\in\mathcal B(J_k)$, with $\\|B_k\\|\leq\\|G\\|$, such that, for all $d_0\in E$, $x\in E^\circ$, and $\alpha\in J_k$, $$\label{eq:repeated-letter} \begin{aligned} G(d_0\otimes x^{\otimes k}\otimes\alpha) &=d_0\otimes x^{\otimes k}\otimes B_k\alpha,\\ G^*(d_0\otimes x^{\otimes k}\otimes\alpha) &=d_0\otimes x^{\otimes k}\otimes B_k^*\alpha. \end{aligned}$$ These are identities in the full space $L^2(\mathcal L)$. The operators $B_k$ are independent of the repeated letter $x$; in particular, $x$ may be any complex centered $L^2$ vector.
  - Explanation: The symmetrized operator preserves word levels and acts on repeated-letter tensors through a tail operator independent of that letter.
- **stmt-lem-full-column** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-full-column)): For every projection $r\in\mathcal L$ with $\tau(r)\le t$, there is $b\in\mathcal L$ with $b=rb$ such that $$\big\\|(G-R_b)\|_{Hr}\big\\|\le c_0\epsilon. \label{eq:full-column}$$ The same assertion holds for $G^*$.
  - Explanation: On a sufficiently small right-supported subspace the operator is close to bounded right multiplication.
- **stmt-lem-propagation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-propagation)): For every $k\ge1$ and $v\in B=L^2(A_0)$, $$\big\\|B_k(s^{\otimes k}\otimes v) -s^{\otimes k}\otimes B_0v\big\\| \le 2c_0\epsilon\\|v\\|_2. \label{eq:propagation}$$ This also holds with $B_k^*$ and $B_0^*$ in place of $B_k$ and $B_0$.
  - Explanation: The tail actions propagate along repeated letters with a uniform error independent of word length.
- **stmt-lem-compressed-commutator** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-compressed-commutator)): For every $h\in e\mathcal Le$, $$\big\\|[J,\iota^*L_h\iota]\big\\| \le (1+4c_0)\epsilon\\|h\\|. \label{eq:compression-bound}$$
  - Explanation: The compression to the assembled word-level subspace preserves a controlled commutator estimate.
- **stmt-lem-dihedral-corner** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dihedral-corner)): The algebra $p\mathcal C p$ is identified with the even functions on the circle by $$\begin{gathered} p\mathcal C p =\{f(w)p:f\in L^\infty(\mathbb T,m),\ f(z)=f(z^{-1})\},\\ \tau_p(f(w)p)=\int_{\mathbb T}f\,dm. \end{gathered} \label{eq:dihedral-corner}$$ The map $f\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}% \mapsto \EndAccSupp{}}f(w)p$ in this formula is a faithful normal unital $*$-isomorphism onto the corner. Moreover, $pDp$ and $p\mathcal C p$ are free with respect to the normalized trace $\tau_p$.
  - Explanation: The dihedral corner is the algebra of even bounded circle functions and is free from the corresponding corner of the factor.
- **stmt-lem-free-compression** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-free-compression)): For $b=b^*\in p\mathcal C p$ with $\tau_p(b)=0$, $$E_{\mathcal C}(ebe)=u^2b,\qquad \\|ebe\\|\le2\sqrt u\,\\|b\\|_{2,\tau_p}+u\\|b\\|, \qquad u=\tau_p(e). \label{eq:free-compression}$$ Here $E_{\mathcal C}$ is the trace-preserving conditional expectation from $\mathcal L$ onto $\mathcal C$.
  - Explanation: Free compression gives a conditional expectation identity and a norm bound involving the normalized trace.
- **stmt-lem-irreducible-hyperfinite** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-irreducible-hyperfinite)): Every separable-predual $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $M$ contains a unital subfactor $R$ with $R'\cap M=\mathbb C1$, generated by an increasing sequence of unital full matrix algebras whose sizes tend to infinity.
  - Explanation: A separable-predual finite factor contains an irreducible subfactor generated by increasing full matrix algebras.
- **stmt-lem-free-position** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-free-position)): Let $Q\subset P$ be a unital irreducible inclusion of $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors, with $P$ of separable predual. For any nonprincipal ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$, there is a subfactor $D\subset Q^\omega$, generated by increasing unital full matrix algebras of unbounded sizes, which is free from the constant copy of $P$ in the tracial ultrapower $P^\omega$.
  - Explanation: The full tracial ultrapower contains an increasing-matrix subfactor free from the constant ambient factor.
- **stmt-prop-finite-copies** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-copies)): Let $M$ be a separable-predual $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor, and let $M$ act on $H_r=L^2(M)^r$ by common left multiplication, where $r\geq1$ is finite. For every $Y\in\mathcal B(H_r)$, every finite $h\geq1$, and every $X\in M_h(M)$, $$\lVert[Y^{(h)},X]\rVert \leq (3c_1+2)\,g_M(Y)\,\lVert X\rVert.$$ Here $X$ denotes its entrywise action on $H_r^h$; the constant is independent of $M,r,h$.
  - Explanation: Matrix commutators on finite standard multiplicities satisfy a bound uniform in the factor and multiplicities.
- **stmt-prop-factor-representations** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-factor-representations)): Let $P\subset\mathcal B(K)$ be a unital factor acting on a separable Hilbert space. For every $Y\in\mathcal B(K)$, every $h\ge1$, and every $X\in M_h(P)$, $$\lVert[Y^{(h)},X]\rVert \le C_{\mathrm{fac}}\,g_P(Y)\,\lVert X\rVert.$$
  - Explanation: The matrix commutator estimate extends to factor representations on separable Hilbert spaces.
- **stmt-prop-separable-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-separable-estimate)): Let $P\subset\mathcal B(K)$ be any unital von Neumann algebra on a separable Hilbert space. Then $$\lVert[Y^{(h)},X]\rVert\le C\,g_P(Y)\,\lVert X\rVert \qquad(Y\in\mathcal B(K),\ X\in M_h(P),\ h\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: Central decomposition extends the commutator estimate to arbitrary unital von Neumann algebras on separable Hilbert spaces.
- **stmt-prop-all-derivations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-all-derivations)): Let $A$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra and $\sigma:A\to\mathcal B(K)$ a unital $*$-representation. Every bounded $\sigma$-derivation $\Delta:A\to\mathcal B(K)$ satisfies $$\lVert\Delta\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le C\lVert\Delta\rVert$$ and is implemented by a bounded operator on $K$.
  - Explanation: Every bounded derivation in a unital adjoint-preserving representation is completely bounded and has a bounded implementer.

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-kirchberg",
        "exact_quote": "For a unital $C^*$-algebra $A$, the following properties are equivalent.*\n\n1.  *Every bounded unital homomorphism $\\pi:A\\to\\mathcal B(H)$, on any Hilbert space $H$, is similar by a bounded invertible operator to a $*$-homomorphism.*\n\n2.  *For every faithful unital $*$-representation $\\sigma:A\\to\\mathcal B(K)$, on any Hilbert space $K$, every bounded linear map $\\Delta:A\\to\\mathcal B(K)$ satisfying $$\\Delta(ab)=\\Delta(a)\\sigma(b)+\\sigma(a)\\Delta(b)$$ has the form $\\Delta(a)=V\\sigma(a)-\\sigma(a)V$ for some $V\\in\\mathcal B(K)$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-288.1"

        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $\\epsilon\\geq0$ and $$\\label{eq:free-corner-hypothesis}\n  \\|[G,L_x]\\|\\leq\\epsilon\\|x\\|\n  \\qquad\n  (x\\in e\\mathcal L e,\n   \\ e\\in D\\text{ a projection with }\\tau(e)=t).$$ If $s=s^*=s^{-1}\\in A_0$ and $\\tau(s)=0$, then $$\\label{eq:free-corner-conclusion}\n \\left\\|P_{L^2(A_0)}[G,L_s]\\big|_{L^2(A_0)}\\right\\|\n       \\leq c_1\\epsilon,\n \\qquad c_1=6\\pi^2(1+4c_0).$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-288.1"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-free-corner",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal L=D*A_0$ be as above, let $t=1/N$ for an integer $N\\geq2$, and suppose that $G\\in\\mathcal B(L^2(\\mathcal L))$ commutes with $L_D$.           "source_id": "canonical:PM-288.1"

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-288.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-word-structure",
        "exact_quote": "The symmetrized operator $G$ preserves every level $\\mathcal H_k$ in <a href=\"#eq:word-decomposition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:word-decomposition\">[eq:word-decomposition]</a>. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Hyperreflexivity** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A von Neumann algebra is *hyperreflexive* if the distance from any operator to the algebra is bounded by a fixed multiple of the supremum of its off-diagonal compression norms over the algebra’s invariant subspaces.
- **Commutator seminorm** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a represented unital von Neumann algebra $P\subset\mathcal B(K)$ and $Y\in\mathcal B(K)$, put $$g_P(Y)=\sup_{a\in P,\,\\|a\\|\le1}\\|Ya-aY\\|,$$ and write $Y^{(h)}$ for the diagonal amplification of $Y$ on $K^h$.
- **Rectangular amplification norms** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For a linear map $u:A\to\mathcal B(K,F)$, its amplification $u_{p,q}$ acts entrywise on $M_{p,q}(A)$, with values in $\mathcal B(K^q,F^p)$. Rectangular matrix norms on $A$ are the usual $C^*$-matrix norms. We abbreviate $u_{h,h}$ to $u_h$, and set $$\\|u\\|_{\mathrm{cb}}=\sup_{h\ge1}\\|u_h\\|,\qquad \\|u\\|_{\mathrm{row}}=\sup_{h\ge1}\\|u_{1,h}\\|,\qquad \\|u\\|_{\mathrm{col}}=\sup_{h\ge1}\\|u_{h,1}\\|.$$ A map is completely bounded when its cb norm is finite.
- **Tracial Hilbert space** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): If $(P,\tau)$ is a finite von Neumann algebra with a faithful normal tracial state, $L^2(P)=L^2(P,\tau)$ is the completion of $P$ for $\langle x,y\rangle=\tau(y^*x)$.
- **Left and right multiplication** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): Left and right multiplication are denoted by $L_a$ and $R_a$, with $R_a\xi=\xi a$; thus $R_aR_b=R_{ba}$.
- **Rectangular derivation** ([source](#eq:rectangular-derivation)): A *rectangular derivation* for $*$-representations $\sigma:A\to\mathcal B(K)$ and $\lambda:A\to\mathcal B(F)$ is a linear map $\Delta:A\to\mathcal B(K,F)$ satisfying $$\Delta(ab)=\Delta(a)\sigma(b)+\lambda(a)\Delta(b) \qquad(a,b\in A). \label{eq:rectangular-derivation}$$
- **Tracial freeness** ([source](#sec:tensors)): Freeness here is scalar tracial freeness: the trace of an alternating product of centered letters from the two algebras is zero.
- **Off-diagonal distance seminorm** ([source](#stmt-cor-hyperreflexivity)): For $T\in\mathcal B(H)$, set $$\alpha_M(T)=\sup\{\\|(1-e)Te\\|:e\in M',\ e=e^*=e^2\}.$$

      "exact_definition": "For a linear map $u:A\\to\\mathcal B(K,F)$, its amplification $u_{p,q}$ acts entrywise on $M_{p,q}(A)$, with values in $\\mathcal B(K^q,F^p)$. Rectangular matrix norms on $A$ are the usual $C^*$-matrix norms. We abbreviate $u_{h,h}$ to $u_h$, and set $$\\|u\\|_{\\mathrm{cb}}=\\sup_{h\\ge1}\\|u_h\\|,\\qquad\n \\|u\\|_{\\mathrm{row}}=\\sup_{h\\ge1}\\|u_{1,h}\\|,\\qquad\n \\|u\\|_{\\mathrm{col}}=\\sup_{h\\ge1}\\|u_{h,1}\\|.$$ A map is completely bounded when its cb norm is finite.",
      "interpretation": "Entrywise rectangular matrix amplifications define the completely bounded, row and column norms.",
      "kind": "notation",
      "name": "Rectangular amplification norms",
      "scope": "Linear maps from a C*-algebra to operators between representation spaces"
    },

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-288.1"

      "interpretation": "The trace defines the Hilbert completion of the algebra.",
      "kind": "definition",

    },

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-288.1"

      "interpretation": "A derivation between domain and codomain representations satisfies the bimodule Leibniz identity.",

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| row-constant | Row and column constant | = | $4\sqrt2$ | Independent of the represented algebra, representation and Hilbert space |
| cyclic-constant | Cyclic implementation constant | = | $(1+4c_{\rm row})^2$ | Independent of the represented algebra, representation and Hilbert space |
| corner-constant | Free-product corner constant | = | $6\pi^2(1+4c_0)$ | Independent of the represented algebra, representation and Hilbert space |
| factor-constant | Factor representation constant | = | $\max\{3c_1+2,\,1+2c_{\mathrm{row}},\,2\}$ | Independent of the represented algebra, representation and Hilbert space |
| global-constant | Universal commutator constant | = | $3C_{\mathrm{fac}}+2$ | Independent of the represented algebra, representation and Hilbert space |
| hyperreflexivity-constant | Universal hyperreflexivity constant | ≤ | $2C$ | Independent of the represented algebra, representation and Hilbert space |
| cyclic-cb-coefficient | Cyclic completely bounded norm coefficient | ≤ | $4c_{\rm row}$ | Independent of the represented algebra, representation and Hilbert space |
| narrow-arc-norm | Narrow-arc compression norm | ≤ | $3u$ | Depends on the normalized corner trace |
| hankel-lower-bound | Hankel operator norm lower bound | ≥ | $\frac1{2\pi^2}$ | Uniform over the admissible corner traces |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **row-column** [claim](#stmt-lem-row-column) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-row-column): Bounded rectangular derivations have uniformly bounded row and column amplification norms. -> Bounded rectangular derivations have uniformly bounded row and column amplification norms.
- **cb-implementation** [claim](#stmt-lem-cb-implementation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cb-implementation): Completely bounded rectangular derivations admit a spatial implementer with a controlled norm. -> Completely bounded rectangular derivations admit a spatial implementer with a controlled norm.
- **cyclic-factorization** [claim](#stmt-lem-cyclic-factorization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cyclic-factorization): In a cyclic representation, an amplified unit vector factors through a unit vector and a contractive column in the represented algebra. -> In a cyclic representation, an amplified unit vector factors through a unit vector and a contractive column in the represented algebra.
- **cyclic-implementation** [claim](#stmt-lem-cyclic-implementation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cyclic-implementation): A bounded rectangular derivation with cyclic domain is completely bounded and spatially implemented. -> A bounded rectangular derivation with cyclic domain is completely bounded and spatially implemented.
- **free-factor** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-factor): The relative commutant of the increasing-matrix factor in the free product is scalar, so the free product is a finite factor. -> The relative commutant of the increasing-matrix factor in the free product is scalar, so the free product is a finite factor.
- **free-symmetrization** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-symmetrization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-symmetrization): Unitary averaging preserves the target compression and corner bounds while imposing the required conjugation symmetries. -> Unitary averaging preserves the target compression and corner bounds while imposing the required conjugation symmetries.
- **invariant-intertwiners** [claim](#stmt-lem-invariant-intertwiners) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-invariant-intertwiners): Invariant intertwiners vanish between distinct tensor levels and are linear combinations of tail permutations on equal levels. -> Invariant intertwiners vanish between distinct tensor levels and are linear combinations of tail permutations on equal levels.
- **word-structure** [claim](#stmt-lem-word-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-word-structure): The symmetrized operator preserves word levels and acts on repeated-letter tensors through a tail operator independent of that letter. -> The symmetrized operator preserves word levels and acts on repeated-letter tensors through a tail operator independent of that letter.
- **full-column** [claim](#stmt-lem-full-column) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-full-column): On a sufficiently small right-supported subspace the operator is close to bounded right multiplication. -> On a sufficiently small right-supported subspace the operator is close to bounded right multiplication.
- **propagation** [claim](#stmt-lem-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-propagation): The tail actions propagate along repeated letters with a uniform error independent of word length. -> The tail actions propagate along repeated letters with a uniform error independent of word length.
- **compressed-commutator** [claim](#stmt-lem-compressed-commutator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compressed-commutator): The compression to the assembled word-level subspace preserves a controlled commutator estimate. -> The compression to the assembled word-level subspace preserves a controlled commutator estimate.
- **dihedral-corner** [claim](#stmt-lem-dihedral-corner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dihedral-corner): The dihedral corner is the algebra of even bounded circle functions and is free from the corresponding corner of the factor. -> The dihedral corner is the algebra of even bounded circle functions and is free from the corresponding corner of the factor.
- **free-compression** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-compression): Free compression gives a conditional expectation identity and a norm bound involving the normalized trace. -> Free compression gives a conditional expectation identity and a norm bound involving the normalized trace.
- **free-corner** [claim](#stmt-thm-free-corner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-compression-2): Small-corner commutator control implies a uniform compressed commutator bound against a centered symmetry in the other free-product algebra. -> Small-corner commutator control implies a uniform compressed commutator bound against a centered symmetry in the other free-product algebra.
- **irreducible-hyperfinite** [claim](#stmt-lem-irreducible-hyperfinite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-irreducible-hyperfinite): A separable-predual finite factor contains an irreducible subfactor generated by increasing full matrix algebras. -> A separable-predual finite factor contains an irreducible subfactor generated by increasing full matrix algebras.
- **free-position** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-position) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-position): The full tracial ultrapower contains an increasing-matrix subfactor free from the constant ambient factor. -> The full tracial ultrapower contains an increasing-matrix subfactor free from the constant ambient factor.
- **finite-copies** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-copies) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-copies): Matrix commutators on finite standard multiplicities satisfy a bound uniform in the factor and multiplicities. -> Matrix commutators on finite standard multiplicities satisfy a bound uniform in the factor and multiplicities.
- **factor-representations** [claim](#stmt-prop-factor-representations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-factor-representations): The matrix commutator estimate extends to factor representations on separable Hilbert spaces. -> The matrix commutator estimate extends to factor representations on separable Hilbert spaces.
- **separable-estimate** [claim](#stmt-prop-separable-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-separable-estimate): Central decomposition extends the commutator estimate to arbitrary unital von Neumann algebras on separable Hilbert spaces. -> Central decomposition extends the commutator estimate to arbitrary unital von Neumann algebras on separable Hilbert spaces.
- **universal** [claim](#stmt-thm-universal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-separable-estimate-2): Matrix commutators are controlled by the scalar commutator seminorm with a universal constant. -> Matrix commutators are controlled by the scalar commutator seminorm with a universal constant.
- **all-derivations** [claim](#stmt-prop-all-derivations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-all-derivations): Every bounded derivation in a unital adjoint-preserving representation is completely bounded and has a bounded implementer. -> Every bounded derivation in a unital adjoint-preserving representation is completely bounded and has a bounded implementer.
- **kirchberg** [claim](#stmt-thm-kirchberg) / [proof](#pm-h-9): The similarity property is equivalent to implementation of bounded derivations in every faithful unital adjoint-preserving representation. -> The similarity property is equivalent to implementation of bounded derivations in every faithful unital adjoint-preserving representation.
- **similarity** [claim](#stmt-thm-similarity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-all-derivations-2): Every bounded unital complex-linear algebra homomorphism from a unital complex C*-algebra is similar to an adjoint-preserving homomorphism by a bounded invertible operator. -> Every bounded unital complex-linear algebra homomorphism from a unital complex C*-algebra is similar to an adjoint-preserving homomorphism by a bounded invertible operator.
- **hyperreflexivity** [claim](#stmt-cor-hyperreflexivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-all-derivations-3): The off-diagonal compression seminorm bounds distance to any unital von Neumann algebra uniformly. -> The off-diagonal compression seminorm bounds distance to any unital von Neumann algebra uniformly.

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-288.1"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-kirchberg",
          "exact_quote": "For a unital $C^*$-algebra $A$, the following properties are equivalent.*\n\n1.  *Every bounded unital homomorphism $\\pi:A\\to\\mathcal B(H)$, on any Hilbert space $H$, is similar by a bounded invertible operator to a $*$-homomorphism.*\n\n2.  *For every faithful unital $*$-representation $\\sigma:A\\to\\mathcal B(K)$, on any Hilbert space $K$, every bounded linear map $\\Delta:A\\to\\mathcal B(K)$ satisfying $$\\Delta(ab)=\\Delta(a)\\sigma(b)+\\sigma(a)\\Delta(b)$$ has the form $\\Delta(a)=V\\sigma(a)-\\sigma(a)V$ for some $V\\in\\mathcal B(K)$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-288.1"
        }

There exists an operator $S\\in\\mathcal B(H)$, invertible with bounded inverse, such that $$\\rho(a)=S\\pi(a)S^{-1}\\qquad(a\\in A)$$ is a $*$-homomorphism. In particular, $\\rho(a^*)=\\rho(a)^*$ for every $a\\in A$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-288.1"
        }

For $T\\in\\mathcal B(H)$, set $$\\alpha_M(T)=\\sup\\{\\|(1-e)Te\\|:e\\in M',\\ e=e^*=e^2\\}.$$ Then, with the absolute constant $C$ from Theorem <a href=\"#thm:universal\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:universal\">2</a>, $$\\label{eq:universal-hyperreflexivity}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(T,M)\\le 2C\\,\\alpha_M(T)\\qquad(T\\in\\mathcal B(H)).$$ Thus every such $M$ is hyperreflexive with constant at most $2C$, independently of the algebra, its representation, and the Hilbert space. No optimality of this constant is asserted.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-288.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-similarity**: A bounded unital complex-linear algebra homomorphism from a unital complex C*-algebra admits a bounded invertible change of coordinates that preserves adjoints.
- **I-amplification**: An absolute constant controls the commutator at every finite matrix amplification by the scalar commutator seminorm and the matrix norm.
- **I-cyclic-domain**: A bounded rectangular derivation with a cyclic domain representation has a bounded spatial implementer and a uniform completely bounded norm estimate.
- **I-distance**: Distance to a represented unital von Neumann algebra is controlled by its off-diagonal compression seminorm with an absolute constant.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-map**: Correct adjoints of an arbitrary bounded linear operator map by similarity. -> The similarity conclusion does not cover a map lacking the required algebra homomorphism hypotheses.
- **A-computation**: Treat the existence of a similarity as a finite algorithm with a prescribed condition number. -> An existence assertion does not specify a finite construction or an a priori condition-number bound.
- **A-cyclic**: Apply the cyclic-domain implementer estimate to a rectangular derivation whose domain has no established cyclic vector. -> The cited implementer estimate has an unmet domain hypothesis.
- **A-hyperreflexivity**: Apply the universal distance estimate to an arbitrary operator system. -> The algebra class required by the corollary has not been established.
- **A-group**: Deduce unitarizability of every uniformly bounded group representation from the similarity theorem. -> The required bounded extension to a group C*-algebra need not exist.
- **A-corner**: Use the free-product corner estimate with an arbitrary test operator in the output algebra. -> The specified compressed commutator test requires a trace-zero self-adjoint unitary.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The similarity theorem asserts existence of a bounded invertible operator and allows it to depend on the whole homomorphism, without an a priori condition-number bound. Its proof uses weak operator cluster limits, tracial and Hilbert space ultrapowers, and passage through arbitrarily many cyclic subspaces. These analytic constructions do not provide a finite input encoding or a finite procedure constructing the similarity. Finite matrix tests of intermediate estimates would not certify the unrestricted existence conclusion.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): Similarity applies to bounded, complex-linear, unital algebra homomorphisms from unital complex C*-algebras on complex Hilbert spaces. Blocked use: ['Applying the conclusion to a merely linear operator map without multiplicativity or to a map with unverified boundedness or unitality.']
- **L2** (nonconstructive): The similarity result asserts existence of a bounded invertible operator; it does not supply an a priori condition-number bound for that homomorphism. Blocked use: ['Treating the existence theorem as a numerical algorithm with a prescribed conditioning guarantee.']
- **L3** (edge_case): The similarity conclusion does not require separability, nuclearity, finite generation, faithfulness, or normality. Blocked use: ['Restricting the main similarity theorem to faithful normal representations or separable spaces merely because intermediate arguments use them.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): The hyperreflexivity conclusion concerns unital von Neumann algebras acting on complex Hilbert spaces. Blocked use: ['Extending the distance estimate to an arbitrary non-self-adjoint operator algebra without the stated algebraic hypothesis.']
- **L5** (unknown): Optimality of the universal hyperreflexivity constant is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Advertising the bound as a sharp or smallest possible constant.']
- **L6** (counterexample_scope): Uniformly bounded group representations need not extend boundedly to a group C*-algebra, so the similarity theorem requires a separate extension check before being applied to them. Blocked use: ['Concluding unitarizability of an arbitrary uniformly bounded group representation directly from algebra-homomorphism similarity.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): The cyclic-domain implementation lemma requires a cyclic vector for the domain representation. Blocked use: ['Invoking that lemma on a noncyclic domain before the global derivation argument.']
- **L8** (approximation): The propagation proof fixes the projection, word level, and vectors before taking a limit; it does not require a convergence rate uniform in the corner trace or word level. Blocked use: ['Extracting a uniform finite truncation or stopping rule from the qualitative vanishing argument.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): Free positioning in the ultrapower assumes an irreducible inclusion of finite factors and separable predual of the ambient factor. Blocked use: ['Applying the free-position lemma to an arbitrary inclusion with nontrivial relative commutant.']
- **L10** (nonconstructive): Free positioning is asserted in a tracial ultrapower using a nonprincipal ultrafilter and increasing matrix algebras of unbounded sizes. Blocked use: ['Treating this ultrapower existence result as a finite construction of a similarity transform.']
- **L11** (unknown): Executable similarity construction, runtime guarantees, and numerical stability guarantees are ABSENT from the source. Blocked use: ['Claiming a deployable solver or a certified floating-point implementation of the unrestricted theorem.']

          "quote": "Let $A$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra, let $\\sigma:A\\to\\mathcal B(K)$ and $\\lambda:A\\to\\mathcal B(F)$ be $*$-representations, and suppose that $\\sigma$ has a cyclic vector. Every bounded rectangular derivation $\\Delta:A\\to\\mathcal B(K,F)$ is completely bounded, with $$\\|\\Delta\\|_{\\mathrm{cb}}\\le4c_{\\rm row}\\|\\Delta\\|.\n \\label{eq:cyclic-cb}$$ Moreover there is $V\\in\\mathcal B(K,F)$ such that $$\\Delta(a)=V\\sigma(a)-\\lambda(a)V\\quad(a\\in A),\\qquad\n \\|V\\|\\le c_0\\|\\Delta\\|.\n \\label{eq:cyclic-implementation}$$",
          "quote_sha256": "bab7cbdd2db0c1fd3a378aef95eaf38fb6b784294230c40f93e812c9e526efe1"
        }
      ],
      "hypotheses": "The domain representation has a cyclic vector and both representations preserve adjoints.",
      "question": "When is a bounded rectangular derivation spatially implemented?",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    }
  ],
  "related_papers": [
    {
      "basis": "stated",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-3",
          "quote": "Unlike the homomorphisms in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:similarity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:similarity\">1</a>, a uniformly bounded group representation need not have a bounded extension to the full or reduced group $C^*$-algebra; the automatic extension is to $\\ell^1(G)$.",

        }

If $\\Delta$ is completely bounded, then some $V\\in\\mathcal B(K,F)$ satisfies $$\\Delta(a)=V\\sigma(a)-\\lambda(a)V\\qquad(a\\in A).\n \\label{eq:rectangular-implementation}$$ If $d=\\|\\Delta\\|>0$, one may choose $$\\|V\\|\\le d\\bigl(1+\\|\\Delta\\|_{\\mathrm{cb}}/d\\bigr)^2.$$",

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 288 (`lean/docs/288.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Kadison's similarity theorem through uniform derivation estimates](../../preprints/Kadisons-similarity-theorem-through-uniform-derivation-estimates-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Kadison's similarity problem asks whether every bounded unital homomorphism from a unital complex $C^*$-algebra into operators on a Hilbert space is similar to a $*$-homomorphism. The formalization proves this for arbitrary Hilbert spaces. It also gives one universal hyperreflexivity constant for all unital von Neumann algebras.
>
> The linked commutator estimate is uniform over all finite matrix amplifications: its constant is independent of the algebra, Hilbert space, and amplification size. This is the quantitative derivation estimate supporting the similarity result.

Comparator source `KadisonSimilarity.lean` (4475 bytes, sha256 `6f8497039c46225fa8181d0f9955a116fec217d855291bfdb475a5ab326cf944`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

namespace KadisonSimilarity

universe u v

section Homomorphisms

variable (A H : Type*) [CStarAlgebra A]
  [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℂ H] [CompleteSpace H]

abbrev BoundedUnitalHom :=
  {π : A →ₐ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H) // Continuous π}

def SimilarToStar (π : BoundedUnitalHom A H) : Prop :=
  ∃ S : (H →L[ℂ] H)ˣ, ∀ a : A,
    (S : H →L[ℂ] H) * π.1 (star a) * (↑S⁻¹ : H →L[ℂ] H) =
      star ((S : H →L[ℂ] H) * π.1 a * (↑S⁻¹ : H →L[ℂ] H))

end Homomorphisms

section Commutators

variable {R : Type*} [NormedRing R]

def commutator (S T : R) : R := S * T - T * S

end Commutators

variable {H : Type u} [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℂ H]

section

attribute [-instance] NonUnitalCStarAlgebra.toNormedSpace

run_cmd Lean.modifyEnv fun env => Lean.Meta.auxLemmasExt.setState env {}

abbrev HilbertCopies (H : Type u) (n : ℕ) := PiLp 2 (fun _ : Fin n => H)

def matrixOperator {n : ℕ} (X : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) (H →L[ℂ] H)) :
    HilbertCopies H n →L[ℂ] HilbertCopies H n :=
  (PiLp.continuousLinearEquiv 2 ℂ (fun _ : Fin n => H)).symm.toContinuousLinearMap.comp
    (ContinuousLinearMap.pi fun i =>
      ∑ j : Fin n, (X i j).comp (PiLp.proj 2 (fun _ : Fin n => H) j))

def amplify (Y : H →L[ℂ] H) (n : ℕ) :
    HilbertCopies H n →L[ℂ] HilbertCopies H n :=
  (PiLp.continuousLinearEquiv 2 ℂ (fun _ : Fin n => H)).symm.toContinuousLinearMap.comp
    (ContinuousLinearMap.pi fun i => Y.comp (PiLp.proj 2 (fun _ : Fin n => H) i))

def innerDerivation (Y : H →L[ℂ] H) :
    (H →L[ℂ] H) →L[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H) :=
  ContinuousLinearMap.mul ℂ (H →L[ℂ] H) Y -
    (ContinuousLinearMap.mul ℂ (H →L[ℂ] H)).flip Y

variable [CompleteSpace H]

def scalarCommutatorNorm (P : VonNeumannAlgebra H) (Y : H →L[ℂ] H) : ℝ := by
  let : SeminormedAddCommGroup P.toStarSubalgebra.toSubalgebra.toSubmodule := inferInstance
  let : NormedSpace ℂ P.toStarSubalgebra.toSubalgebra.toSubmodule := inferInstance
  exact ‖(innerDerivation Y).comp P.toStarSubalgebra.toSubalgebra.toSubmodule.subtypeL‖

def CommutatorEstimate (P : VonNeumannAlgebra H) (C : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (Y : H →L[ℂ] H) (n : ℕ) (X : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) (H →L[ℂ] H)),
    (∀ i j, X i j ∈ P) →
    ‖commutator (amplify Y n) (matrixOperator X)‖ ≤
      C * scalarCommutatorNorm P Y * ‖matrixOperator X‖

def UniformCommutatorEstimate (C : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (K : Type u) [NormedAddCommGroup K] [InnerProductSpace ℂ K] [CompleteSpace K]
    (P : VonNeumannAlgebra K), CommutatorEstimate P C

def UniversalCommutatorTheorem : Prop := ∃ C : ℝ, 0 ≤ C ∧ UniformCommutatorEstimate.{u} C

def SimilarityTheorem : Prop :=
  ∀ (A : Type u) [CStarAlgebra A] (K : Type v) [NormedAddCommGroup K]
    [InnerProductSpace ℂ K] [CompleteSpace K] (π : BoundedUnitalHom A K),
    SimilarToStar A K π

abbrev CommutantProjection (M : VonNeumannAlgebra H) :=
  {e : H →L[ℂ] H // e ∈ M.commutant ∧ star e = e ∧ e * e = e}

def offDiagonalSeminorm (M : VonNeumannAlgebra H) (T : H →L[ℂ] H) : ℝ :=
  sSup (Set.range fun e : CommutantProjection M =>
    ‖(1 - (e : H →L[ℂ] H)) * T * (e : H →L[ℂ] H)‖)

def UniversalHyperreflexivity (C : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (K : Type u) [NormedAddCommGroup K] [InnerProductSpace ℂ K] [CompleteSpace K]
    (M : VonNeumannAlgebra K) (T : K →L[ℂ] K),
    Metric.infDist T (M : Set (K →L[ℂ] K)) ≤ 2 * C * offDiagonalSeminorm M T

end

run_cmd Lean.modifyEnv fun env => Lean.Meta.auxLemmasExt.setState env {}

def rowConstant : ℝ := 4 * Real.sqrt 2
def cyclicConstant : ℝ := (1 + 4 * rowConstant) ^ 2
def cornerConstant : ℝ := 6 * Real.pi ^ 2 * (1 + 4 * cyclicConstant)
def factorConstant : ℝ := max (max (3 * cornerConstant + 2) (1 + 2 * rowConstant)) 2
def universalConstant : ℝ := 3 * factorConstant + 2

/-- One absolute constant controls every finite matrix commutator. -/
theorem universalCommutatorTheorem : UniversalCommutatorTheorem.{u} := by
  sorry

/-- Every von Neumann algebra has hyperreflexivity constant at most `2 * universalConstant`. -/
theorem universalHyperreflexivity : UniversalHyperreflexivity.{u} universalConstant := by
  sorry

/-- Every bounded unital representation is similar to a star representation. -/
theorem similarityTheorem : SimilarityTheorem.{u,v} := by
  sorry

end KadisonSimilarity

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every bounded complex-linear unital algebra homomorphism from a unital complex <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra into the bounded operators on an arbitrary Hilbert space is similar to a $`*`$-homomorphism. This resolves Kadison's similarity conjecture positively. We also obtain one universal hyperreflexivity constant for all unital von Neumann algebras on arbitrary complex Hilbert spaces.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Kadison’s similarity problem asks whether the adjoint operation of a $C^*$-algebra is respected, after a bounded change of Hilbert space coordinates, by every bounded representation of its underlying algebra. Kadison formulated the problem in 1955 in his study of the orthogonalization of operator representations (Kadison 1955) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Kadison1955](#ref-Kadison1955)]. Its force lies in the absence of any assumed relation between the algebraic representation and the involution: multiplicativity and boundedness alone must produce one equivalent Hilbert space inner product for which all represented adjoints are correct. This paper resolves the problem positively in its unrestricted bounded unital form.

<a id="stmt-thm-similarity"></a>
<div id="thm:similarity" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Similarity). *Let $A$ be any unital complex $C^*$-algebra, let $H$ be any complex Hilbert space, and let $\pi:A\to\mathcal B(H)$ be a bounded, complex-linear, unital algebra homomorphism. There exists an operator $S\in\mathcal B(H)$, invertible with bounded inverse, such that $$\rho(a)=S\pi(a)S^{-1}\qquad(a\in A)$$ is a $*$-homomorphism. In particular, $\rho(a^*)=\rho(a)^*$ for every $a\in A$.*

</div>

The theorem imposes no separability, nuclearity, finite generation, faithfulness, or normality condition. The similarity may depend on the whole homomorphism. The result concerns the existence of this single bounded invertible operator, and does not require an a priori bound for its condition number.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Historical context

The nuclear case was established by Bunce and Christensen (Bunce 1981; Christensen 1981) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Bunce1981](#ref-Bunce1981), [Christensen1981](#ref-Christensen1981)]; see also the precise quantitative comparison in Pisier (2006, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Pisier2006Nuclear](#ref-Pisier2006Nuclear)]. Haagerup’s theorem that every nuclear $C^*$-algebra is amenable (Haagerup 1983a, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Haagerup1983Amenable](#ref-Haagerup1983Amenable)] connects this case with the cohomology of operator algebras. Complete boundedness provided the principal analytic framework for the general similarity problem. Haagerup solved the cyclic representation case and identified complete boundedness as the criterion for similarity to a $*$-representation (Haagerup 1983b) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Haagerup1983](#ref-Haagerup1983)]. He also proved the similarity property for $C^*$-algebras without tracial states (Haagerup 1983b, Proposition 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Haagerup1983](#ref-Haagerup1983)], and Christensen established it for type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors with property $\Gamma$ (Christensen 1986) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Christensen1986](#ref-Christensen1986)]; see also Ozawa (2006, sec. 1.2, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Ozawa2006](#ref-Ozawa2006)]. These cases explain the role of the finite-factor argument below, where the estimate must be uniform in the factor and its representation multiplicity.

Christensen established the corresponding criterion for derivations: a derivation into the ambient bounded operators is inner precisely when it is completely bounded (Christensen 1977) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Christensen1977](#ref-Christensen1977)]. Paulsen’s similarity theorem extends the homomorphism criterion to unital operator algebras and gives the optimal condition number (Paulsen 1984, 226) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Paulsen1984](#ref-Paulsen1984)]. Kirchberg then proved that, for each unital $C^*$-algebra, the similarity property is equivalent to innerness of derivations in every faithful $*$-representation (Kirchberg 1996) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Kirchberg1996](#ref-Kirchberg1996)]; we use the explicit per-algebra formulation in Ozawa (2006, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Ozawa2006](#ref-Ozawa2006)]. Ringrose’s automatic continuity theorem allows this derivation property to be stated using bounded derivations (Ringrose 1972) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Ringrose1972](#ref-Ringrose1972)].

A von Neumann algebra is *hyperreflexive* if the distance from any operator to the algebra is bounded by a fixed multiple of the supremum of its off-diagonal compression norms over the algebra’s invariant subspaces. More recently, Eleftherakis and Paulsen (2026, Theorem 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[EleftherakisPaulsen2026](#ref-EleftherakisPaulsen2026)] characterized a positive answer to the similarity problem by preservation of hyperreflexivity when adjoining a projection: whenever $M\subset\mathcal B(H)$ is a hyperreflexive von Neumann algebra and $q\in\mathcal B(H)$ is a projection, $W^*(M,q)$ is hyperreflexive. Their result is an equivalence with this extension property and with the assertion that every von Neumann algebra is hyperreflexive. Corollary <a href="#cor:hyperreflexivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:hyperreflexivity">3</a> below makes the latter consequence quantitative with one universal constant.

Pisier organized quantitative similarity estimates through the similarity degree and its interpretation as the length of matrix factorizations (Pisier 1999, 2001) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Pisier1999Degree](#ref-Pisier1999Degree), [Pisier2001Remarks](#ref-Pisier2001Remarks)]. His characterization of nuclearity by a similarity exponent below three distinguishes the strength of the nuclear case (Pisier 2006, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Pisier2006Nuclear](#ref-Pisier2006Nuclear)]. These results concern quantitative bounds for homomorphisms. The proof below is organized around a uniform bound for derivations, and concludes with existence of a similarity as stated in Theorem <a href="#thm:similarity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:similarity">1</a>.

The companion result on discrete groups identifies unitarizability with amenability (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[OpenAIUnitarizability2026](#ref-OpenAIUnitarizability2026)]. Unlike the homomorphisms in Theorem <a href="#thm:similarity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:similarity">1</a>, a uniformly bounded group representation need not have a bounded extension to the full or reduced group $C^*$-algebra; the automatic extension is to $\ell^1(G)$.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The uniform estimate and proof strategy

An inner derivation is a map $a\mathrel{%
  \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}%
  \mapsto
  \EndAccSupp{}}[Y,a]$. Complete boundedness means uniform boundedness of all entrywise finite matrix amplifications. Our main estimate controls those amplifications of an inner derivation by its norm at the scalar level. For a represented unital von Neumann algebra $P\subset\mathcal B(K)$ and $Y\in\mathcal B(K)$, put $$g_P(Y)=\sup_{a\in P,\,\|a\|\le1}\|Ya-aY\|,$$ and write $Y^{(h)}$ for the diagonal amplification of $Y$ on $K^h$.

<a id="stmt-thm-universal"></a>
<div id="thm:universal" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:universal"></a>
**Theorem 2** (Uniform commutator estimate). *There is an absolute constant $C<\infty$ such that, for every complex Hilbert space $K$, every unital von Neumann algebra $P\subset\mathcal B(K)$, every $Y\in\mathcal B(K)$, every integer $h\ge1$, and every $X\in M_h(P)$, $$\label{eq:universal}
  \|[Y^{(h)},X]\|\le C\,g_P(Y)\,\|X\|.$$ Here $M_h(P)$ acts naturally on $K^h$, and $[T,U]=TU-UT$.*

</div>

<a id="stmt-cor-hyperreflexivity"></a>
<div id="cor:hyperreflexivity" class="corollary">

<a id="eq:universal-hyperreflexivity"></a>
**Corollary 3** (Universal hyperreflexivity). *Let $H$ be any complex Hilbert space and let $M\subset\mathcal B(H)$ be a unital von Neumann algebra. For $T\in\mathcal B(H)$, set $$\alpha_M(T)=\sup\{\|(1-e)Te\|:e\in M',\ e=e^*=e^2\}.$$ Then, with the absolute constant $C$ from Theorem <a href="#thm:universal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:universal">2</a>, $$\label{eq:universal-hyperreflexivity}
 \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(T,M)\le 2C\,\alpha_M(T)\qquad(T\in\mathcal B(H)).$$ Thus every such $M$ is hyperreflexive with constant at most $2C$, independently of the algebra, its representation, and the Hilbert space. No optimality of this constant is asserted.*

</div>

Applying the commutator estimate to $M'$ gives this distance bound through Arveson’s commutant-distance formula. The proof is given in Section <a href="#sec:hyperreflexivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hyperreflexivity">7.5</a>.

The difficult part is obtaining a constant independent of matrix size for finite factors. Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> first combines Dickson’s row estimate (Dickson 2014, Lemma 2.9) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Dickson2014](#ref-Dickson2014)] with its column counterpart to implement rectangular derivations with cyclic domain. Here rectangular derivations take values in operators between two representation spaces. The implementation norm is controlled by the derivation norm with an absolute constant.

Sections <a href="#sec:tensors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tensors">3</a>–<a href="#sec:hankel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hankel">5</a> use this cyclic-domain estimate in a tracial free product $\mathcal L=D*A_0$, where $D$ is a ${\rm II}_1$ factor generated by increasing full matrix algebras and $A_0$ is a finite von Neumann algebra with a faithful normal tracial state. The input is an operator $G$ on $L^2(\mathcal L)$ commuting with left multiplication by $D$, whose commutators with elements of a corner $e\mathcal L e$ have norm at most $\epsilon$ times the element norm. Here $e\in D$ is a projection of trace $1/N$, for an integer $N\ge2$. The output bounds the compression to $L^2(A_0)$ of its commutator with any trace-zero self-adjoint unitary in $A_0$, by an absolute multiple of $\epsilon$ independent of the corner trace. This is Theorem <a href="#thm:free-corner" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:free-corner">9</a>.

The reduced-word model belongs to the free-product framework developed by Ching and Voiculescu (Ching 1973; Voiculescu 1985) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Ching1973](#ref-Ching1973), [Voiculescu1985](#ref-Voiculescu1985)]. Its role here is to turn the corner hypothesis into a statement about operators on Hilbert tensor products.

In Section <a href="#sec:tensors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tensors">3</a>, averaging over the finite matrix unitary groups reveals the structure of the operator on reduced words. Finite-dimensional Schur–Weyl duality supplies invariant cycle-trace coefficients; the endpoint $L^1$ bound and boundedness on Hilbert tensor products eliminate all contractions except permutations between equally many source and test letters. At each word level, repeated-letter tensors consequently carry an action on their remaining factors that is independent of the repeated $D$-letter. Section <a href="#sec:propagation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:propagation">4</a> approximates $G$, on each sufficiently small right support, by a bounded right multiplier. This propagates the repeated-letter action through arbitrarily long words with a uniform error. Section <a href="#sec:hankel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hankel">5</a> compresses a multiplication operator built from two free trace-zero self-adjoint unitaries. The resulting Hankel matrix has norm bounded below as the corner trace tends to zero. The trace factors in the upper and lower bounds cancel, completing the free-product corner estimate.

Section <a href="#sec:finite-factors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite-factors">6</a> uses Popa’s relative independence theorem (Popa 2014, Theorem 0.1(a)) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Popa2014](#ref-Popa2014)] to place this free product inside the tracial ultrapower of an arbitrary separable-predual ${\rm II}_1$ factor after matrix amplification. The irreducible-inclusion case already appears in Popa’s free-independent sequence construction (Popa 1995, Theorem 2.1 and proof of Corollary 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Popa1995](#ref-Popa1995)]. The operator under study is passed through a Hilbert space ultrapower and compressed to the tracial $L^2$-space, preserving the necessary commutator coefficients. Section <a href="#sec:global" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:global">7</a> extends the estimate to arbitrary normal factor representations, then uses central decomposition and separable reducing subspaces to obtain Theorem <a href="#thm:universal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:universal">2</a>. The resulting constant is numerical and independent of matrix size, representation, and Hilbert space. Finally, using the cyclic-domain estimate again, finite collections of cyclic columns of a $*$-representation implement a given derivation locally. Their scalar commutator norms are uniformly bounded, so <a href="#eq:universal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:universal">[eq:universal]</a> gives complete boundedness of the full derivation. Paulsen’s theorem implements it, and Kirchberg’s equivalence proves Theorem <a href="#thm:similarity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:similarity">1</a>.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Rows, cyclic modules, and derivations

This section supplies two inputs to the proof: a uniform implementer for a rectangular derivation with cyclic domain, and the per-algebra derivation criterion that will conclude similarity. The cyclic estimate will first be used to extend control from a small free-product corner to an entire right-supported subspace.

All Hilbert spaces and algebras are complex, and inner products are linear in the first variable. All algebra homomorphisms are complex-linear, and all $*$-representations are unital unless stated otherwise. No separability or faithfulness is implicit. We write $E_1$ for the closed unit ball of a normed space $E$. For a unital self-adjoint operator algebra $P\subset\mathcal B(K)$ and $Y\in\mathcal B(K)$, set <a id="eq:commutator-seminorm"></a>$$g_P(Y)=\sup_{a\in P_1}\|Ya-aY\|.
 \label{eq:commutator-seminorm}$$ The same notation will be used when $P$ is a specified represented copy of an abstract algebra.

For a linear map $u:A\to\mathcal B(K,F)$, its amplification $u_{p,q}$ acts entrywise on $M_{p,q}(A)$, with values in $\mathcal B(K^q,F^p)$. Rectangular matrix norms on $A$ are the usual $C^*$-matrix norms. We abbreviate $u_{h,h}$ to $u_h$, and set $$\|u\|_{\mathrm{cb}}=\sup_{h\ge1}\|u_h\|,\qquad
 \|u\|_{\mathrm{row}}=\sup_{h\ge1}\|u_{1,h}\|,\qquad
 \|u\|_{\mathrm{col}}=\sup_{h\ge1}\|u_{h,1}\|.$$ A map is completely bounded when its cb norm is finite. For an operator $Y$, the notation $Y^{(h)}$ means the diagonal operator on $h$ copies of its Hilbert space.

If $(P,\tau)$ is a finite von Neumann algebra with a faithful normal tracial state, $L^2(P)=L^2(P,\tau)$ is the completion of $P$ for $\langle x,y\rangle=\tau(y^*x)$. We write $\|\cdot\|_2$ for this norm, and $\|x\|_1=\tau(|x|)$ for $x\in P$. Left and right multiplication are denoted by $L_a$ and $R_a$, with $R_a\xi=\xi a$; thus $R_aR_b=R_{ba}$. The tracial commutation theorem identifies $L(P)'$ with $\{R_a:a\in P\}$. The support notation for an $L^2$-vector is understood through its affiliated operator: if $\xi=v|\xi|$ is its polar decomposition, its left and right support projections are $vv^*$ and $v^*v$, respectively. They are equivalent in $P$, and in particular have the same trace. For a projection $r\in P$, the condition that $R_b$ vanish on $L^2(P)(1-r)$ is exactly $b=rb$. We use these standard facts, and standard-form theory for von Neumann algebras, without separability restrictions (Takesaki 1979; Haagerup 1975) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Takesaki1979](#ref-Takesaki1979), [Haagerup1975](#ref-Haagerup1975)].

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Uniform row and column estimates

<a id="eq:dickson-row"></a>
Dickson’s argument derives the row estimate from Haagerup’s little Grothendieck inequality and credits Christensen with the estimate in this form (Dickson 2014, Lemmas 2.8–2.9) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Dickson2014](#ref-Dickson2014)]; this is part of the factorization approach to similarity developed by Pisier (Pisier 1999, 2001) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Pisier1999Degree](#ref-Pisier1999Degree), [Pisier2001Remarks](#ref-Pisier2001Remarks)]. The estimate states that every bounded unital homomorphism $\varphi:A\to\mathcal B(K)$, for any unital $C^*$-algebra $A$, satisfies $$\|\varphi\|_{\mathrm{row}}
       \le\sqrt2\,\|\varphi\|^2
 \qquad\text{\citep[Lemma~2.9, Equation~(19)]{Dickson2014}}.
 \label{eq:dickson-row}$$ The map $\varphi^\sharp(a)=\varphi(a^*)^*$ is complex-linear and has the same norm as $\varphi$. It is again a homomorphism, since $$\varphi^\sharp(ab)
 =\bigl(\varphi(b^*)\varphi(a^*)\bigr)^*
 =\varphi^\sharp(a)\varphi^\sharp(b).$$ Apply <a href="#eq:dickson-row" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dickson-row">[eq:dickson-row]</a> to $\varphi^\sharp$ on the row $[a_1^*,\ldots,a_h^*]$, and take the rectangular adjoint. This gives the same bound for $\|\varphi\|_{\mathrm{col}}$. Consequently we fix the absolute constant <a id="eq:row-constant"></a>$$c_{\rm row}=4\sqrt2,
 \label{eq:row-constant}$$ which bounds both norms whenever $\|\varphi\|\le2$.

<a id="eq:rectangular-derivation"></a>
Let $A$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra. A *rectangular derivation* for $*$-representations $\sigma:A\to\mathcal B(K)$ and $\lambda:A\to\mathcal B(F)$ is a linear map $\Delta:A\to\mathcal B(K,F)$ satisfying $$\Delta(ab)=\Delta(a)\sigma(b)+\lambda(a)\Delta(b)
 \qquad(a,b\in A).
 \label{eq:rectangular-derivation}$$ The derivation identity and unitality imply $\Delta(1)=0$.

<a id="stmt-lem-row-column"></a>
<div id="lem:row-column" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:rectangular-row-column"></a>
**Lemma 4** (Row and column bounds). *Every bounded rectangular derivation satisfies $$\max\{\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{row}},\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{col}}\}
       \le c_{\rm row}\|\Delta\|.
 \label{eq:rectangular-row-column}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-row-column"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-row-column</summary>

<a id="eq:triangular-homomorphism"></a>
*Proof.* The assertion is immediate when $\Delta=0$. Otherwise put $d=\|\Delta\|>0$, and consider the upper triangular map $$\Phi(a)=
 \begin{pmatrix}
  \lambda(a)&d^{-1}\Delta(a)\\
  0&\sigma(a)
 \end{pmatrix}
 \in\mathcal B(F\oplus K).
 \label{eq:triangular-homomorphism}$$ It is unital and multiplicative by <a href="#eq:rectangular-derivation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rectangular-derivation">[eq:rectangular-derivation]</a>. Its diagonal map is contractive, and its off-diagonal map has norm one; hence $\|\Phi\|\le2$. The off-diagonal rectangular compression of $\Phi_{p,q}$, from $K^q$ to $F^p$, is $d^{-1}\Delta_{p,q}$. The row and column estimates for $\Phi$ give <a href="#eq:rectangular-row-column" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rectangular-row-column">[eq:rectangular-row-column]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Completely bounded implementation

<a id="eq:cyclic-constant"></a>
We use the quantitative completely bounded similarity theorem of Paulsen: if a unital homomorphism $\varphi:A\to\mathcal B(H)$ is completely bounded, there is an invertible $S\in\mathcal B(H)$ such that $S\varphi(\cdot)S^{-1}$ is completely contractive and $\|S\|\|S^{-1}\|\le\|\varphi\|_{\mathrm{cb}}$ (Paulsen 1984, 226) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Paulsen1984](#ref-Paulsen1984)]. For a unital $C^*$-algebra the resulting homomorphism is a $*$-homomorphism: a unitary and its inverse are sent to contractions, so the image is a unitary; unitaries linearly span the algebra. For the later cyclic-domain estimate, set $$c_0=(1+4c_{\rm row})^2.
 \label{eq:cyclic-constant}$$

<a id="stmt-lem-cb-implementation"></a>
<div id="lem:cb-implementation" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:rectangular-implementation"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Completely bounded rectangular derivations). *Let $\sigma,\lambda,\Delta$ satisfy <a href="#eq:rectangular-derivation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rectangular-derivation">[eq:rectangular-derivation]</a>. If $\Delta$ is completely bounded, then some $V\in\mathcal B(K,F)$ satisfies $$\Delta(a)=V\sigma(a)-\lambda(a)V\qquad(a\in A).
 \label{eq:rectangular-implementation}$$ If $d=\|\Delta\|>0$, one may choose $$\|V\|\le d\bigl(1+\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{cb}}/d\bigr)^2.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-cb-implementation"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-cb-implementation</summary>

<a id="eq:intertwining-metric"></a>
<a id="eq:metric-blocks"></a>
*Proof.* We use the triangular-metric argument recalled in Ozawa (2006, Lemma 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Ozawa2006](#ref-Ozawa2006)], allowing different domain and codomain representations and keeping track of the implementer norm. For $\Delta=0$, take $V=0$. Otherwise the map $\Phi$ in <a href="#eq:triangular-homomorphism" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:triangular-homomorphism">[eq:triangular-homomorphism]</a> is completely bounded, with $$\|\Phi\|_{\mathrm{cb}}
 \le 1+\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{cb}}/d=:G.$$ Choose the similarity $S$ supplied by Paulsen’s theorem, and put $D=S^*S$. The fact that $S\Phi(\cdot)S^{-1}$ preserves adjoints is equivalent to $$D\Phi(a)=\Phi(a^*)^*D\qquad(a\in A).
 \label{eq:intertwining-metric}$$ Write $D=(D_{ij})_{i,j=1}^2$ relative to $F\oplus K$. Its first row in <a href="#eq:intertwining-metric" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intertwining-metric">[eq:intertwining-metric]</a> gives $$\begin{aligned}
 D_{11}\lambda(a)&=\lambda(a)D_{11},\notag\\
 d^{-1}D_{11}\Delta(a)+D_{12}\sigma(a)&=\lambda(a)D_{12}.
 \label{eq:metric-blocks}
\end{aligned}$$ Since $D\ge\|D^{-1}\|^{-1}I$, its compression $D_{11}$ is positive invertible on $F$, with $\|D_{11}^{-1}\|\le\|D^{-1}\|$. Its inverse also commutes with $\lambda(A)$. Hence $$V=-dD_{11}^{-1}D_{12}$$ satisfies <a href="#eq:rectangular-implementation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rectangular-implementation">[eq:rectangular-implementation]</a>, by <a href="#eq:metric-blocks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:metric-blocks">[eq:metric-blocks]</a>, and $$\|V\|
 \le d\,\|D^{-1}\|\|D\|
 =d\,\bigl(\|S^{-1}\|\|S\|\bigr)^2
 \le dG^2.$$ If either Hilbert space is zero, $\Delta=0$, so it has already been covered. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Cyclic domains

The cyclic hypothesis needed below concerns only the domain $*$-representation. The output representation may have arbitrary multiplicity.

<a id="stmt-lem-cyclic-factorization"></a>
<div id="lem:cyclic-factorization" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (Column factorization in a cyclic representation). *Let $P\subset\mathcal B(K)$ be a unital von Neumann algebra with a cyclic vector. For every integer $h\ge1$ and every unit vector $\xi=(\xi_1,\ldots,\xi_h)\in K^h$, there are a unit vector $\zeta\in K$ and a contraction $v=(v_i)\in M_{h,1}(P)$ such that $\xi_i=v_i\zeta$ for every $i$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-cyclic-factorization"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-cyclic-factorization</summary>

*Proof.* Put $N=P'$, and choose a cyclic vector $\Omega$ for $P$. It separates $N$: if $x\in N$ and $x\Omega=0$, then $xa\Omega=ax\Omega=0$ for every $a\in P$, whence $x=0$. Let $L=\overline{N\Omega}\subset K$. This subspace reduces $N$, and restriction $r:N\to\mathcal B(L)$ is faithful and normal. Indeed, an element in its kernel annihilates $\Omega$; normality follows from compression to a reducing subspace. Thus it is a normal $*$-isomorphism onto the von Neumann algebra $r(N)$, and its inverse is normal. The vector $\Omega$ is cyclic and separating for $r(N)$.

The standard-form theorem for a representation with a cyclic separating vector realizes every normal positive functional on $r(N)$ as a vector functional, with its vector in the natural cone (Haagerup 1975, Lemma 2.10(1), pp. 278–279) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Haagerup1975](#ref-Haagerup1975)]. Apply it, through the normal isomorphism $r$, to $$\omega(x)=\sum_{i=1}^h\langle x\xi_i,\xi_i\rangle,
 \qquad x\in N.$$ We obtain $\zeta\in L\subset K$ such that $\langle x\zeta,\zeta\rangle=\omega(x)$ for every $x\in N$. In particular $\|\zeta\|^2=\omega(1)=1$. The assignment $$x\zeta\mathrel{%
  \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=27FC}%
  \begingroup
    \let\mathrel{%
  \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}%
  \mapsto
  \EndAccSupp{}}\mapsto
    \let\longrightarrow\longrightarrow
    \longmapsto
  \endgroup
  \EndAccSupp{}}(x\xi_1,\ldots,x\xi_h),\qquad x\in N,$$ is isometric, since both squared norms equal $\omega(x^*x)$. It extends to an isometry from $\overline{N\zeta}$ into $K^h$. Extend it by zero on $\overline{N\zeta}^{\perp}$, obtaining a contraction $v:K\to K^h$. The subspace $\overline{N\zeta}$ reduces $N$, and the isometry intertwines the $N$-actions. Therefore $vx=x^{(h)}v$ for every $x\in N$. Each coordinate $v_i$ lies in $N'=P$, and $v\zeta=\xi$, as required. All these constructions, including the standard-form assertion, are valid on nonseparable Hilbert spaces. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-cyclic-implementation"></a>
<div id="lem:cyclic-implementation" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:cyclic-cb"></a>
<a id="eq:cyclic-implementation"></a>
**Lemma 7** (Implementation on a cyclic domain). *Let $A$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra, let $\sigma:A\to\mathcal B(K)$ and $\lambda:A\to\mathcal B(F)$ be $*$-representations, and suppose that $\sigma$ has a cyclic vector. Every bounded rectangular derivation $\Delta:A\to\mathcal B(K,F)$ is completely bounded, with $$\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{cb}}\le4c_{\rm row}\|\Delta\|.
 \label{eq:cyclic-cb}$$ Moreover there is $V\in\mathcal B(K,F)$ such that $$\Delta(a)=V\sigma(a)-\lambda(a)V\quad(a\in A),\qquad
 \|V\|\le c_0\|\Delta\|.
 \label{eq:cyclic-implementation}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-cyclic-implementation"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-cyclic-implementation</summary>

*Proof.* Zero spaces and the zero derivation are immediate, so assume they do not occur. Put $P=\sigma(A)''$. Fix $h\ge1$, $X\in M_h(A)_1$, and a unit vector $\xi\in K^h$. Lemma <a href="#lem:cyclic-factorization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cyclic-factorization">6</a> gives a unit vector $\zeta\in K$ and a contraction $v\in M_{h,1}(P)$ with $v\zeta=\xi$.

<a id="eq:cyclic-column-lifts"></a>
Matrix Kaplansky density gives a net of column contractions $z_\alpha\in M_{h,1}(\sigma(A))$ converging strongly to $v$. To see the rectangular assertion directly, regard $v$ as the first column of a matrix in $M_h(P)$, with all other columns zero. Approximate that matrix strongly by contractions in $M_h(\sigma(A))$, and compress to its first input coordinate. The $*$-image $\sigma(A)$ is the $C^*$-quotient $A/\ker\sigma$, and the same quotient identification holds at each square matrix level. Consequently each $z_\alpha$ lifts to $w_\alpha\in M_{h,1}(A)$ with $$\|w_\alpha\|\le2,\qquad
 \sigma_{h,1}(w_\alpha)=z_\alpha,\qquad
 \sigma_{h,1}(w_\alpha)\zeta\longrightarrow\xi.
 \label{eq:cyclic-column-lifts}$$ For example, first lift the square matrix whose first column is $z_\alpha$ and whose other columns vanish to a matrix of norm less than $2$, then compress its first column. Only fixed slack in the quotient norm is being used.

Entrywise application of <a href="#eq:rectangular-derivation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rectangular-derivation">[eq:rectangular-derivation]</a> gives $$\Delta_h(X)\sigma_{h,1}(w_\alpha)
   =\Delta_{h,1}(Xw_\alpha)
       -\lambda_h(X)\Delta_{h,1}(w_\alpha).$$ Since $\lambda$ is completely contractive, Lemma <a href="#lem:row-column" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:row-column">4</a> and <a href="#eq:cyclic-column-lifts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cyclic-column-lifts">[eq:cyclic-column-lifts]</a> imply $$\bigl\|\Delta_h(X)\sigma_{h,1}(w_\alpha)\zeta\bigr\|
 \le c_{\rm row}\|\Delta\|
       \bigl(\|Xw_\alpha\|+\|\lambda_h(X)\|\|w_\alpha\|\bigr)
 \le4c_{\rm row}\|\Delta\|.$$ At the fixed finite level $h$, $\Delta_h(X)$ is already a bounded operator. We may therefore take the limit in <a href="#eq:cyclic-column-lifts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cyclic-column-lifts">[eq:cyclic-column-lifts]</a>, obtaining the same bound on $\|\Delta_h(X)\xi\|$. Taking the supremum over $\xi,X,h$ proves <a href="#eq:cyclic-cb" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cyclic-cb">[eq:cyclic-cb]</a>. The quotient argument did not require $\Delta$ to vanish on $\ker\sigma$; its identity was applied to the chosen lifts in $A$. Lemma <a href="#lem:cb-implementation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cb-implementation">5</a> and <a href="#eq:cyclic-constant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cyclic-constant">[eq:cyclic-constant]</a> now give <a href="#eq:cyclic-implementation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cyclic-implementation">[eq:cyclic-implementation]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The derivation criterion for similarity

For the last step of the proof we use Kirchberg’s equivalence in its per-algebra form. We state the quantifiers because the final $*$-representation used to test derivations is independent of the bounded homomorphism in the similarity problem.

<a id="stmt-thm-kirchberg"></a>
<div id="thm:kirchberg" class="theorem">

**Theorem 8** (Kirchberg’s similarity–derivation equivalence). *For a unital $C^*$-algebra $A$, the following properties are equivalent.*

1.  *Every bounded unital homomorphism $\pi:A\to\mathcal B(H)$, on any Hilbert space $H$, is similar by a bounded invertible operator to a $*$-homomorphism.*

2.  *For every faithful unital $*$-representation $\sigma:A\to\mathcal B(K)$, on any Hilbert space $K$, every bounded linear map $\Delta:A\to\mathcal B(K)$ satisfying $$\Delta(ab)=\Delta(a)\sigma(b)+\sigma(a)\Delta(b)$$ has the form $\Delta(a)=V\sigma(a)-\sigma(a)V$ for some $V\in\mathcal B(K)$.*

</div>

This is the theorem of Kirchberg (1996) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Kirchberg1996](#ref-Kirchberg1996)]; the exact formulation with every faithful representation is stated in Ozawa (2006, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Ozawa2006](#ref-Ozawa2006)]. The usual derivation property is stated without a continuity hypothesis. Ringrose’s automatic continuity theorem for derivations from $C^*$-algebras into Banach bimodules (Ringrose 1972, Theorem 2, p. 434) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Ringrose1972](#ref-Ringrose1972)] makes it equivalent to the bounded version above. The triangular metric computation in Lemma <a href="#lem:cb-implementation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cb-implementation">5</a> uses only Paulsen’s established completely bounded theorem; the unrestricted similarity conclusion will follow from Theorem <a href="#thm:kirchberg" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:kirchberg">8</a> after all bounded derivations have been treated.

<a id="sec:tensors"></a>
### Free-product geometry and invariant tensors

Let $D$ be a ${\rm II}_1$ factor generated as a von Neumann algebra by an increasing sequence $(D_j)$ of unital full matrix subalgebras whose sizes tend to infinity. Let $A_0$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with a faithful normal tracial state, and form the tracial von Neumann free product $$\mathcal L=D*A_0.$$ We use $\tau$ for the free-product trace and its restrictions. Freeness here is scalar tracial freeness: the trace of an alternating product of centered letters from the two algebras is zero. Its Hilbert-space consequence is the orthogonal decomposition into reduced words, with each reduced-word space isometric to the corresponding Hilbert tensor product. This model comes from the free-product framework of Ching (1973) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Ching1973](#ref-Ching1973)] and Voiculescu (1985) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Voiculescu1985](#ref-Voiculescu1985)]. The corner estimate below is proved here from that word decomposition.

This section and the next two prove the following estimate. Its constant does not depend on the trace of the corner.

<a id="stmt-thm-free-corner"></a>
<div id="thm:free-corner" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:free-corner-hypothesis"></a>
<a id="eq:free-corner-conclusion"></a>
**Theorem 9** (Free-product corner estimate). *Let $\mathcal L=D*A_0$ be as above, let $t=1/N$ for an integer $N\geq2$, and suppose that $G\in\mathcal B(L^2(\mathcal L))$ commutes with $L_D$. Suppose that $\epsilon\geq0$ and $$\label{eq:free-corner-hypothesis}
  \|[G,L_x]\|\leq\epsilon\|x\|
  \qquad
  (x\in e\mathcal L e,
   \ e\in D\text{ a projection with }\tau(e)=t).$$ If $s=s^*=s^{-1}\in A_0$ and $\tau(s)=0$, then $$\label{eq:free-corner-conclusion}
 \left\|P_{L^2(A_0)}[G,L_s]\big|_{L^2(A_0)}\right\|
       \leq c_1\epsilon,
 \qquad c_1=6\pi^2(1+4c_0).$$*

</div>

Since equal-trace projections in $D$ are unitarily conjugate, commutation with $L_D$ makes it equivalent to assume <a href="#eq:free-corner-hypothesis" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:free-corner-hypothesis">[eq:free-corner-hypothesis]</a> for one such projection. Moreover, for $x\in e\mathcal L e$ the commutator in that equation is supported on $eL^2(\mathcal L)$. The proof of the theorem is completed after the Fourier compression calculation in Section <a href="#sec:hankel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hankel">5</a>.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Factoriality and symmetrization

<a id="stmt-lem-free-factor"></a>
<div id="lem:free-factor" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10**. *One has $D'\cap\mathcal L=\mathbb C1$. In particular, $\mathcal L$ is a ${\rm II}_1$ factor.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-free-factor"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-free-factor</summary>

*Proof.* Put $E=L^2(D)$, $E^\circ=E\ominus\mathbb C1$, and $B^\circ=L^2(A_0)\ominus\mathbb C1$. Regrouping reduced words by their first and last $A_0$-letters gives the $D$–$D$ bimodule decomposition $$L^2(\mathcal L)\ominus L^2(D)
 \cong E\otimes\mathcal V\otimes E,
 \qquad
 \mathcal V=
 \bigoplus_{n\geq1}
 B^\circ\otimes(E^\circ\otimes B^\circ)^{\otimes(n-1)}.$$ The endpoints in $E$ are unrestricted; expanding each as $\mathbb C1\oplus E^\circ$ recovers every reduced word containing an $A_0$-letter exactly once. Left and right $D$ act on the first and last factors, respectively.

Choose a Haar unitary $v$ in a diffuse abelian subalgebra of $D$ and put $u_n=v^n$. These unitaries converge ultraweakly to zero in $D$. Indeed, the restriction of any normal functional to $W^*(v)\cong L^\infty(\mathbb T)$ has an $L^1(\mathbb T)$ density, whose Fourier coefficients tend to zero. Consequently $L_{u_n}$ and $R_{u_n^*}$ converge weakly to zero on $E$: first test against bounded vectors in $D$, and then use their $L^2$ density. On elementary tensors the matrix coefficients of $$L_{u_n}R_{u_n^*}
  =L_{u_n}\otimes I_{\mathcal V}\otimes R_{u_n^*}$$ therefore tend to zero. The same holds on the whole complement by density and uniform boundedness. A $D$-central vector in this complement is fixed by all these operators, and hence is zero.

If $z\in D'\cap\mathcal L$, its component perpendicular to $L^2(D)$ is $D$-central, because the displayed decomposition is a bimodule decomposition. Thus $z=E_D(z)\in D$, where $E_D$ is the trace-preserving conditional expectation. It follows that $z\in Z(D)=\mathbb C1$. The center of $\mathcal L$ is contained in this relative commutant. Its faithful finite trace and its diffuse subfactor $D$ then make $\mathcal L$ a ${\rm II}_1$ factor. ◻

</details>
